/**\\n34:  * The interface for a source reader which is responsible for reading the records from the source\\n35:  * splits assigned by {@link SplitEnumerator}.\\n36:  *\\n37:  * <p>For most non-trivial source reader, it is recommended to use {@link\\n38:  * org.apache.flink.connector.base.source.reader.SourceReaderBase SourceReaderBase} which provides\\n39:  * an efficient hand-over protocol to avoid blocking I/O inside the task thread and supports various\\n40:  * split-threading models.\\n41:  *\\n42:  * <p>Implementations can provide the following metrics:\\n43:  *\\n44:  * <ul>\\n45:  *   <li>{@link OperatorIOMetricGroup#getNumRecordsInCounter()} (highly recommended)\\n46:  *   <li>{@link OperatorIOMetricGroup#getNumBytesInCounter()} (recommended)\\n47:  *   <li>{@link SourceReaderMetricGroup#getNumRecordsInErrorsCounter()} (recommended)\\n48:  *   <li>{@link SourceReaderMetricGroup#setPendingRecordsGauge(Gauge)}\\n49:  *   <li>{@link SourceReaderMetricGroup#setPendingBytesGauge(Gauge)}\\n50:  * </ul>\\n51:  *\\n52:  * @param <T> The type of the record emitted by this source reader.\\n53:  * @param <SplitT> The type of the source splits.\\n54:  */\\n55: @Public\\n56: public interface SourceReader<T, SplitT extends SourceSplit>\\n57:         extends AutoCloseable, CheckpointListener {\\n58: \\n59:     /** Start the reader. */\\n60:     void start();\\n61: \\n62:     /**\\n63:      * Poll the next available record into the {@link ReaderOutput}.\\n64:      *\\n65:      * <p>The implementation must make sure this method is non-blocking.\\n66:      *\\n67:      * <p>Although the implementation can emit multiple records into the given ReaderOutput, it is\\n68:      * recommended not doing so. Instead, emit one record into the ReaderOutput and return a {@link\\n69:      * InputStatus#MORE_AVAILABLE} to let the caller thread know there are more records available.\\n70:      *\\n71:      * @return The InputStatus of the SourceReader after the method invocation.\\n72:      */\\n73:     InputStatus pollNext(ReaderOutput<T> output) throws Exception;\\n74: \\n75:     /**\\n76:      * Checkpoint on the state of the source.\\n77:      *\\n78:      * @return the state of the source.\\n79:      */\\n80:     List<SplitT> snapshotState(long checkpointId);\\n81: \\n82:     /**\\n83:      * Returns a future that signals that data is available from the reader.\\n84:      *\\n85:      * <p>Once the future completes, the runtime will keep calling the {@link\\n86:      * #pollNext(ReaderOutput)} method until that method returns a status other than {@link\\n87:      * InputStatus#MORE_AVAILABLE}. After that, the runtime will again call this method to obtain\\n88:      * the next future. Once that completes, it will again call {@link #pollNext(ReaderOutput)} and\\n89:      * so on.\\n90:      *\\n91:      * <p>The contract is the following: If the reader has data available, then all futures\\n92:      * previously returned by this method must eventually complete. Otherwise the source might stall\\n93:      * indefinitely.\\n94:      *\\n95:      * <p>It is not a problem to have occasional \\\"false positives\\\", meaning to complete a future\\n96:      * even if no data is available. However, one should not use an \\\"always complete\\\" future in\\n97:      * cases no data is available, because that will result in busy waiting loops calling {@code\\n98:      * pollNext(...)} even though no data is available.\\n99:      *\\n100:      * @return a future that will be completed once there is a record available to poll.\\n101:      */\\n102:     CompletableFuture<Void> isAvailable();\\n103: \\n104:     /**\\n105:      * Adds a list of splits for this reader to read. This method is called when the enumerator\\n106:      * assigns a split via {@link SplitEnumeratorContext#assignSplit(SourceSplit, int)} or {@link\\n107:      * SplitEnumeratorContext#assignSplits(SplitsAssignment)}.\\n108:      *\\n109:      * @param splits The splits assigned by the split enumerator.\\n110:      */\\n111:     void addSplits(List<SplitT> splits);\\n112: \\n113:     /**\\n114:      * This method is called when the reader is notified that it will not receive any further\\n115:      * splits.\\n116:      *\\n117:      * <p>It is triggered when the enumerator calls {@link\\n118:      * SplitEnumeratorContext#signalNoMoreSplits(int)} with the reader's parallel subtask.\\n119:      */\\n120:     void notifyNoMoreSplits();\\n121: \\n122:     /**\\n123:      * Handle a custom source event sent by the {@link SplitEnumerator}. This method is called when\\n124:      * the enumerator sends an event via {@link SplitEnumeratorContext#sendEventToSourceReader(int,\\n125:      * SourceEvent)}.\\n126:      *\\n127:      * <p>This method has a default implementation that does nothing, because most sources do not\\n128:      * require any custom events.\\n129:      *\\n130:      * @param sourceEvent the event sent by the {@link SplitEnumerator}.\\n131:      */\\n132:     default void handleSourceEvents(SourceEvent sourceEvent) {}\\n133: \\n134:     /**\\n135:      * We have an empty default implementation here because most source readers do not have to\\n136:      * implement the method.\\n137:      *\\n138:      * @see CheckpointListener#notifyCheckpointComplete(long)\\n139:      */\\n140:     @Override\\n141:     default void notifyCheckpointComplete(long checkpointId) throws Exception {}\\n142: \\n143:     /**\\n144:      * Pauses or resumes reading of individual source splits.\\n145:      *\\n146:      * <p>Note that no other methods can be called in parallel, so updating subscriptions can be\\n147:      * done atomically. This method is simply providing connectors with more expressive APIs the\\n148:      * opportunity to update all subscriptions at once.\\n149:      *\\n150:      * <p>This is currently used to align the watermarks of splits, if watermark alignment is used\\n151:      * and the source reads from more than one split.\\n152:      *\\n153:      * <p>The default implementation throws an {@link UnsupportedOperationException} where the\\n154:      * default implementation will be removed in future releases. To be compatible with future\\n155:      * releases, it is recommended to implement this method and override the default implementation.\\n156:      *\\n157:      * @param splitsToPause the splits to pause\\n158:      * @param splitsToResume the splits to resume\\n159:      */\\n160:     @PublicEvolving\\n161:     default void pauseOrResumeSplits(\\n162:             Collection<String> splitsToPause, Collection<String> splitsToResume) {\\n163:         throw new UnsupportedOperationException(\\n164:                 \\\"This source reader does not support pausing or resuming splits which can lead to unaligned splits.\\\\n\\\"\\n165:                         + \\\"Unaligned splits are splits where the output watermarks of the splits have diverged more than the allowed limit.\\\\n\\\"\\n166:                         + \\\"It is highly discouraged to use unaligned source splits, as this leads to unpredictable\\\\n\\\"\\n167:                         + \\\"watermark alignment if there is more than a single split per reader. It is recommended to implement pausing splits\\\\n\\\"\\n168:                         + \\\"for this source. records from the source\n35:  * splits assigned by {@link SplitEnumerator}.\n36:  *\n37:  * <p>For most non-trivial source reader, it is recommended to use {@link\n38:  * org.apache.flink.connector.base.source.reader.SourceReaderBase SourceReaderBase} which provides\n39:  * an efficient hand-over protocol to avoid blocking I/O inside the task thread and supports various\n40:  * split-threading models.\n41:  *\n42:  * <p>Implementations can provide the following metrics:\n43:  *\n44:  * <ul>\n45:  *   <li>{@link OperatorIOMetricGroup#getNumRecordsInCounter()} ReaderOutput}.\n64:      *\n65:      * <p>The implementation must make sure this method is non-blocking.\n66:      *\n67:      * <p>Although the implementation can emit multiple records into the given ReaderOutput, it is\n68:      * recommended not doing so. Instead, emit one record into the ReaderOutput and return a {@link\n69:      * InputStatus#MORE_AVAILABLE} to let the caller thread know there are more records available.\n70:      *\n71:      * @return The InputStatus of the SourceReader after the method invocation.\n72:      */\n73:     InputStatus pollNext(ReaderOutput<T> output) throws Exception;\n74: \n75:     /**\n76:      * Checkpoint on the state of the source.\n77:      *\n78:      * @return the state of the source.\n79:      */\n80:     List<SplitT> snapshotState(long checkpointId);\n81: \n82:     /**\n83:      * Returns a future that signals that data is available from the reader.\n84:      *\n85:      * <p>Once the future completes, the runtime will keep calling the {@link\n86:      * #pollNext(ReaderOutput)} method until that method returns a status other than {@link\n87:      * InputStatus#MORE_AVAILABLE}. After that, the runtime will again call this method to obtain\n88:      * the next future. Once that completes, it will again call {@link #pollNext(ReaderOutput)} and\n89:      * so on.\n90:      *\n91:      * <p>The contract is the following: If the reader has data available, then all futures\n92:      * previously returned by this method must eventually complete. Otherwise the source might stall\n93:      * indefinitely.\n94:      *\n95:      * <p>It is not a problem to have occasional \"false positives\", meaning to complete a future\n96:      * even if no data is available. The implementation could either read from a single split\\n30:  * or from multiple splits.\\n31:  *\\n32:  * @param <E> the element type.\\n33:  * @param <SplitT> the split type.\\n34:  */\\n35: @PublicEvolving\\n36: public interface SplitReader<E, SplitT extends SourceSplit> extends AutoCloseable {\\n37: \\n38:     /**\\n39:      * Fetch elements into the blocking queue for the given splits. The fetch call could be blocking\\n40:      * but it should get unblocked when {@link #wakeUp()} is invoked. In that case, the\\n41:      * implementation may either decide to return without throwing an exception, or it can just\\n42:      * throw an interrupted exception. In either case, this method should be reentrant, meaning that\\n43:      * the next fetch call should just resume from where the last fetch call was waken up or\\n44:      * interrupted. It is up to the implementer to either read all the records of the split or to\\n45:      * stop reading them at some point (for example when a given threshold is exceeded). In that\\n46:      * later case, when fetch is called again, the reading should restart at the record where it\\n47:      * left off based on the {@code SplitState}.\\n48:      *\\n49:      * @return the Ids of the finished splits.\\n50:      * @throws IOException when encountered IO errors, such as deserialization failures.\\n51:      */\\n52:     RecordsWithSplitIds<E> fetch() throws IOException;\\n53: \\n54:     /**\\n55:      * Handle the split changes. Kafka-Flink Streaming Data Flow Architecture Analysis\n\n## Files Examined\n\n### Apache Kafka (github.com/sg-benchmarks/kafka--be816b82)\n\n**Producer API:**\n- `clients/src/main/java/org/apache/kafka/clients/producer/ProducerRecord.java` — Data structure for topic, partition, key, value, timestamp, headers\n- `clients/src/main/java/org/apache/kafka/common/serialization/Serializer.java` — Interface: serialize(String topic, T data) → byte[]\n\n**Consumer API:**\n- `clients/src/main/java/org/apache/kafka/clients/consumer/ConsumerRecord.java` — Data structure for topic, partition, offset, key, value, timestamp, headers\n- `clients/src/main/java/org/apache/kafka/common/serialization/Deserializer.java` — Interface: deserialize(String topic, byte[] data) → T\n- `clients/src/main/java/org/apache/kafka/clients/consumer/OffsetAndMetadata.java` — Wraps offset + optional metadata for commit operations (commitSync(Map<TopicPartition, OffsetAndMetadata>))\n\n### Apache Flink (github.com/apache/flink)\n\n**Core Source API:**\n- `flink-core-api/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/api/connector/dsv2/Source.java` — Placeholder for DataStream V2 source (experimental)\n- `flink-core/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/api/connector/source/SourceReader.java` — Interface: pollNext(ReaderOutput) → InputStatus, snapshotState(checkpointId) → List<SplitT>, addSplits(List<SplitT>), isAvailable()\n- `flink-core/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/api/connector/source/SplitEnumerator.java` — Interface: handleSplitRequest(), addSplitsBack(), snapshotState(checkpointId) → CheckpointT\n\n**Connector Base Framework:**\n- `flink-connectors/flink-connector-base/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/connector/base/source/reader/SourceReaderBase.java` — Abstract base for SourceReader; manages SplitReader instances, buffers records in FutureCompletingBlockingQueue<RecordsWithSplitIds<E>>, coordinates RecordEmitter\n- `flink-connectors/flink-connector-base/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/connector/base/source/reader/splitreader/SplitReader.java` — Interface: fetch() → RecordsWithSplitIds<E>, handleSplitsChanges(SplitsChange), wakeUp()\n- `flink-connectors/flink-connector-base/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/connector/base/source/reader/RecordEmitter.java` — Interface: emitRecord(E element, SourceOutput<T> output, SplitStateT splitState)\n\n**Serialization Boundary:**\n- `flink-core/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/api/common/serialization/DeserializationSchema.java` — Interface: deserialize(byte[] message) → T; used for Kafka consumer record deserialization (custom serde boundary)\n\n**Runtime Integration:**\n- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/streaming/api/operators/SourceOperator.java` — Streaming operator that wraps SourceReader<OUT, SplitT>; calls sourceReader.snapshotState(checkpointId) in snapshotState() method (line 659)\n\n---\n\n## Dependency Chain & Data Flow\n\n### 1. Kafka Producer Path (Write Side)\n\n```\nUser Code\n    ↓\nProducerRecord<K, V>\n    ├─ topic: String\n    ├─ partition: Integer (optional)\n    ├─ key: K (optional)\n    ├─ value: V\n    ├─ timestamp: Long (optional)\n    └─ headers: Iterable<Header>\n    ↓\nSerializer<K> + Serializer<V>\n    ├─ serialize(String topic, T data) → byte[]\n    └─ configure(Map<String, ?> configs, boolean isKey)\n    ↓\nKafkaProducer<K, V>\n    ├─ send(ProducerRecord) → Future<RecordMetadata>\n    ├─ Internal: NetworkClient, Accumulator (batching)\n    ├─ Compression: snappy, lz4, zstd, gzip\n    └─ Transactional: initTransactions(), beginTransaction(), commitTransaction()\n    ↓\nKafka Broker (Topic → Partition → LogSegment)\n```\n\n**Key Contract:** Serializer.serialize() must be deterministic and idempotent. ProducerRecord includes timestamp (CREATE_TIME vs LOG_APPEND_TIME).\n\n---\n\n### 2. Kafka Consumer Path (Read Side)\n\n```\nKafka Broker (Partition → Fetch Offset Range)\n    ↓\nKafkaConsumer<K, V>\n    ├─ subscribe(Collection<String> topics) / assign(Collection<TopicPartition>)\n    ├─ poll(Duration timeout) → ConsumerRecords<K, V>\n    ├─ commitSync(Map<TopicPartition, OffsetAndMetadata>) [blocking]\n    ├─ commitAsync(Map<TopicPartition, OffsetAndMetadata>, Callback) [non-blocking]\n    ├─ Internal: NetworkClient, Fetcher, Deserializer\n    └─ Consumer Group Coordination: rebalance listener, committed offsets in __consumer_offsets topic\n    ↓\nDeserializer<K> + Deserializer<V>\n    ├─ deserialize(String topic, byte[] data) → T\n    ├─ deserialize(String topic, Headers headers, byte[] data) → T\n    └─ configure(Map<String, ?> configs, boolean isKey)\n    ↓\nConsumerRecord<K, V>\n    ├─ topic: String\n    ├─ partition: int\n    ├─ offset: long\n    ├─ key: K (optional, null if key was null on producer)\n    ├─ value: V\n    ├─ timestamp: long\n    ├─ timestampType: TimestampType (CREATE_TIME, LOG_APPEND_TIME)\n    ├─ serializedKeySize: int\n    ├─ serializedValueSize: int\n    └─ headers: Headers\n    ↓\nOffsetAndMetadata\n    ├─ offset: long\n    ├─ metadata: String (optional context)\n    └─ leaderEpoch: Optional<Integer> (for log truncation detection)\n    ↓\ncommitSync(Map<TopicPartition, OffsetAndMetadata>)\n    → Writes to __consumer_offsets topic (broker-side)\n    → Consumer group membership tracked in GroupCoordinator\n    ↓\nUser Code\n```\n\n**Key Contract:** Consumer.commitSync() is blocking. Offsets are only safe when committed. Consumer group rebalancing triggers ConsumerRebalanceListener callbacks.\n\n---\n\n### 3. Flink Source API Integration\n\n```\nSplitEnumerator<SplitT, CheckpointT>\n    ├─ Runs on JobManager (or Coordinator thread pool)\n    ├─ Discovers splits: handleSplitRequest(subtaskId, hostname)\n    ├─ Assigns splits: context.assignSplit(split, subtaskId)\n    ├─ Snapshots state: snapshotState(checkpointId) → CheckpointT\n    └─ Notifies completion: context.signalNoMoreSplits(subtaskId)\n    ↓\nSourceReader<T, SplitT extends SourceSplit>\n    ├─ Runs on TaskManager (per parallel subtask)\n    ├─ Adds splits: addSplits(List<SplitT>)\n    ├─ Polls records: pollNext(ReaderOutput<T>) → InputStatus\n    ├─ Snapshots state: snapshotState(checkpointId) → List<SplitT>\n    ├─ Signals completion: notifyNoMoreSplits()\n    └─ Availability: isAvailable() → CompletableFuture<Void>\n    ↓\nSourceReaderBase<E, T, SplitT, SplitStateT> (connector-base)\n    ├─ Manages: SplitFetcherManager<E, SplitT> (thread pool)\n    ├─ Buffers: FutureCompletingBlockingQueue<RecordsWithSplitIds<E>>\n    ├─ Emits: RecordEmitter<E, T, SplitStateT>.emitRecord()\n    └─ Poll loop:\n    │   ├─ SplitFetcher threads call SplitReader<E, SplitT>.fetch()\n    │   ├─ Records enqueued to elementsQueue\n    │   └─ Main thread calls pollNext() to drain queue → emitRecord()\n    ↓\nSplitReader<E, SplitT>\n    ├─ Reads from external source (e.g., KafkaConsumer)\n    ├─ fetch() → RecordsWithSplitIds<E> [may block]\n    ├─ handleSplitsChanges(SplitsChange) [non-blocking]\n    └─ wakeUp() [interrupt fetch() on blocking I/O]\n    ↓\nRecordEmitter<E, T, SplitStateT>\n    └─ emitRecord(E element, SourceOutput<T> output, SplitStateT splitState)\n        → Converts element to T\n        → Emits to SourceOutput with timestamp/watermark\n        → Updates splitState (e.g., offset tracking)\n    ↓\nReaderOutput<T> / SourceOutput<T>\n    └─ collect(T record)\n    └─ emitWatermark(Watermark)\n    ↓\nStreaming Task (SourceOperator)\n```\n\n**Key Contract:** SourceReader.pollNext() must be non-blocking. isAvailable() returns a future that completes when data is ready. Splits are immutable snapshots; SplitState tracks mutable position (e.g., offset).\n\n---\n\n### 4. Flink-Kafka Connector Bridge (Conceptual)\n\nThe Flink Kafka Connector (in apache/flink-connector-kafka) implements:\n\n```\nKafkaSource<K, V> implements Source<ConsumerRecord<K, V>>\n    ├─ Creates: KafkaSplitEnumerator\n    │   ├─ Uses: KafkaConsumer to discover partitions\n    │   ├─ Creates: KafkaPartitionSplit (topic, partition, startOffset, endOffset)\n    │   └─ Assigns splits to KafkaSourceReader instances\n    └─ Creates: KafkaSourceReader<K, V>\n        ├─ Wraps: KafkaConsumer<K, V>\n        ├─ Implements: SourceReaderBase or SplitReader\n        ├─ Deserializes: ConsumerRecord<K, V> via Kafka Deserializer<K>, Deserializer<V>\n        │   ↓ (Dual serde boundary:)\n        │   1. Kafka layer: Deserializer<K>, Deserializer<V>\n        │   2. Flink layer: DeserializationSchema<ConsumerRecord<K, V>> (optional wrapper)\n        ├─ Tracks: offset per partition in SplitState\n        └─ Emits: ConsumerRecord<K, V> to downstream\n```\n\n**The Dual Serialization Boundary:**\n- **Kafka Serde:** Deserializer<K> and Deserializer<V> convert bytes to typed objects (K, V)\n- **Flink Serde:** DeserializationSchema<T> further transforms ConsumerRecord<K, V> if needed (e.g., extract timestamp, watermark, schema evolution)\n\n---\n\n### 5. Checkpoint-Offset Integration\n\n```\nSourceOperator<OUT, SplitT> (Streaming Runtime)\n    ├─ Holds: SourceReader<OUT, SplitT>\n    ├─ Stores: ListState<byte[]> splitStates\n    └─ On checkpoint:\n        ↓\n        snapshotState(StateSnapshotContext context)\n            checkpointId = context.getCheckpointId()\n            readerState.update(sourceReader.snapshotState(checkpointId))\n            ↓\n            Flink writes readerState to RocksDB/JobManager state backend\n            ↓\n        sourceReader.snapshotState(checkpointId) [via SourceReaderBase]\n            →  SourceReaderBase.snapshotState()\n                ├─ Calls RecordEmitter.getState() to flush pending records\n                ├─ Collects all SplitState snapshots\n                │   └─ For KafkaSourceReader: offset per partition\n                └─ Returns List<SplitT> with serialized state\n            ↓\n        StateBackend (RocksDB, in-memory, external storage)\n            ↓\n        Checkpoint barrier propagates downstream\n            ↓\n        After checkpoint completes: notifyCheckpointComplete(checkpointId)\n            ├─ KafkaSourceReader extracts offsets from SplitState\n                └─ Calls: kafkaConsumer.commitSync(Map<TopicPartition, OffsetAndMetadata>)\n                    ├─ Topic: __consumer_offsets\n                    ├─ GroupId: Flink job's consumer group\n                    ├─ Stores: TopicPartition → (offset, metadata)\n                    └─ Metadata: checkpoint ID or Flink-specific context\n                ↓\n        On recovery:\n            readerState.get() → List<SplitT>\n            sourceReader.addSplits(splits)\n            ├─ KafkaSourceReader seeks to offsets from splits\n            └─ Resume from exactly those offsets\n```\n\n**Key Contract:**\n1. **Snapshot:** SourceReader.snapshotState(checkpointId) returns split state (offsets)\n2. **Barrier:** Checkpoint barrier ensures no in-flight records between snapshots\n3. **Commit:** Post-checkpoint, KafkaConsumer.commitSync() persists offsets to broker\n4. **Recovery:** On restart, Flink restores splits and seeks KafkaConsumer to committed offsets\n5. **Exactly-once:** Only offsets from completed checkpoints are committed to Kafka; failed checkpoints don't advance the committed offset\n\n---\n\n## Detailed Analysis\n\n### Producer Side (Data Ingestion)\n\n**Kafka's Responsibility:**\n- ProducerRecord encapsulates topic, key, value, partition, timestamp\n- Serializer interface defines contract for converting objects to bytes\n- KafkaProducer handles batching, compression, retries, and acknowledgments (acks=all, acks=1, acks=0)\n- Records are replicated to leader and replicas before ack\n\n**Timestamp Semantics:**\n- CREATE_TIME: Producer assigns timestamp; broker uses it as-is\n- LOG_APPEND_TIME: Broker overwrites with append timestamp (prevents clock skew)\n- Message format v2+ includes timestamp type and value in the record\n\n---\n\n### Consumer Side (Data Reading)\n\n**Kafka's Responsibility:**\n- Consumer group coordinates via broker's GroupCoordinator (heartbeat, rebalance)\n- Partitions assigned by PartitionAssignor (round-robin, range, sticky, cooperative)\n- Fetch protocol retrieves messages in offset order from each partition\n- Offset commit writes to __consumer_offsets (internal compacted topic)\n- ConsumerRecord preserves all metadata: offset, partition, timestamp, headers\n\n**Consumer Offset Management:**\n- Manual commit: commitSync() or commitAsync() with explicit offset\n- Automatic commit: enable.auto.commit=true, auto.commit.interval.ms\n- Rebalance behavior: ConsumerRebalanceListener.onPartitionsRevoked() (revoke offset), onPartitionsAssigned() (assign)\n- Log truncation detection: leaderEpoch in ConsumerRecord and OffsetAndMetadata\n\n---\n\n### Flink's Abstraction Layer\n\n**Flink Source API (Core Concepts):**\n\n1. **Splits:** Immutable work units discovered by SplitEnumerator\n   - For Kafka: KafkaPartitionSplit(topic, partition, startOffset, endOffset)\n   - Example: topic=\"events\", partition=0, startOffset=1000, endOffset=5000\n\n2. **SplitEnumerator:** Runs once, discovers all splits, assigns to readers\n   - Assigns splits to readers via SplitEnumeratorContext.assignSplit()\n   - Notifies completion: signalNoMoreSplits(subtaskId)\n   - Checkpoint: saves which splits are unassigned\n\n3. **SourceReader:** Runs per parallel subtask, reads assigned splits\n   - Non-blocking pollNext() method (returns InputStatus)\n   - Futures-based availability: isAvailable() → CompletableFuture<Void>\n   - Stateful: snapshotState() returns mutable state (e.g., current offset)\n\n4. **SourceReaderBase:** Helper class managing async fetch threads\n   - SplitFetcherManager: pool of fetcher threads, each with a SplitReader\n   - FutureCompletingBlockingQueue: bridges async fetch → sync poll\n   - RecordEmitter: transforms fetched elements to output type\n\n5. **RecordEmitter:** User-implemented record conversion\n   - Takes intermediate element E from SplitReader\n   - Converts to final type T and emits via SourceOutput\n   - Updates SplitState (e.g., offset) based on element\n\n---\n\n### Dual Serialization Boundary\n\n**Kafka Layer (Transport):**\n- Bytes → ConsumerRecord<K, V> via Kafka Deserializer<K>, Deserializer<V>\n- Deserializer.deserialize(String topic, byte[] data) is stateless\n- Low-level: only converts bytes to typed objects\n\n**Flink Layer (Semantic):**\n- ConsumerRecord<K, V> → custom type T via DeserializationSchema<T>\n- DeserializationSchema.deserialize(byte[] message) has access to full context\n- Optional: used for schema evolution, filtering, watermark assignment\n\n**Example Integration:**\n```java\n// Kafka deserializes: byte[] → (K key, V value)\nKafkaConsumer<String, JsonNode> consumer = new KafkaConsumer<>(\n    props,\n    new StringDeserializer(),\n    new JsonDeserializer()\n);\n\n// Flink's KafkaSourceReader extracts ConsumerRecord<String, JsonNode>\n// Then applies custom schema:\nnew DeserializationSchema<TradeRecord>() {\n    public TradeRecord deserialize(byte[] message) {\n        JsonNode json = mapper.readValue(message);\n        return new TradeRecord(json.get(\"id\"), json.get(\"price\"));\n    }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n### Checkpoint-Offset Integration Deep Dive\n\n**Phase 1: Snapshot (During Checkpoint)**\n\n```\nJobManager issues checkpoint request\n    ↓ Barrier injected at source\nSourceOperator.snapshotState(StateSnapshotContext)\n    ├─ Calls: sourceReader.snapshotState(checkpointId)\n    │   └─ SourceReaderBase.snapshotState():\n    │       ├─ Pauses SplitFetcherManager threads (briefly)\n    │       ├─ Drains any buffered elements (elementsQueue)\n    │       ├─ Calls RecordEmitter.flushState()\n    │       └─ Collects List<SplitT> with current state\n    │           └─ For Kafka: KafkaPartitionSplit(topic, partition, offset)\n    │               where offset = highest offset processed + 1\n    ├─ Serializes List<SplitT> using SimpleVersionedSerializer<SplitT>\n    └─ Writes to ListState<byte[]> (state backend)\n    ↓\nStateBackend (RocksDB) writes snapshot to stable storage\n    ├─ Local: RocksDB (replica redundancy)\n    └─ Remote: HDFS, S3, DFS (for fault tolerance)\n    ↓\nCheckpoint reaches operator DAG\n    └─ All operators snapshot state\n    ↓\nMaster acknowledges checkpoint complete\n```\n\n**Phase 2: Commit (Post-Checkpoint)**\n\n```\nCoordinator calls: SourceOperator.notifyCheckpointComplete(checkpointId)\n    ↓\nSourceReaderBase.notifyCheckpointComplete(checkpointId)\n    ├─ Extracts offsets from recent splits\n    │   └─ KafkaPartitionSplit.splitId() = \"partition-X:offset-Y\"\n    ├─ Builds: Map<TopicPartition, OffsetAndMetadata>\n    │   ├─ TopicPartition(topic=\"events\", partition=0)\n    │   ├─ OffsetAndMetadata(offset=5001, metadata=\"checkpoint-123\")\n    │   └─ ... (one entry per partition being read)\n    └─ Calls: kafkaConsumer.commitSync(offsets)\n        ├─ Sends OffsetCommit request to broker\n        ├─ Broker writes to __consumer_offsets topic\n        └─ Returns when replicated (durability guarantee)\n    ↓\nConsumer group offset updated\n    └─ Next restart can resume from committed offset\n```\n\n**Phase 3: Recovery**\n\n```\nFlink TaskManager restarts (on failure)\n    ↓\nSourceOperator.initializeState(StateInitializationContext)\n    ├─ Restores ListState<byte[]> from state backend\n    ├─ Deserializes: List<SplitT>\n    │   └─ KafkaPartitionSplit(topic, partition, offset=5001)\n    ├─ Calls: sourceReader.addSplits(splits)\n    │   ├─ KafkaSourceReader.addSplits():\n    │   │   ├─ Creates KafkaConsumer if not exists\n    │   │   ├─ Calls: consumer.assign(topicPartitions)\n    │   │   ├─ Calls: consumer.seek(tp, offset)\n    │   │   └─ Resumes polling from offset\n    │   └─ SourceReaderBase enqueues to internal queue\n    ↓\nProcessing resumes from offset 5001\n    └─ No duplicate records (offset 5001 was not yet emitted before failure)\n```\n\n**Why This Works:**\n1. **Snapshots before emit:** The offset in the snapshot is the next-to-read offset (not yet emitted)\n2. **No double-counting:** If a record at offset 5000 was fetched but not emitted before failure, it's re-emitted after recovery\n3. **Atomic commits:** Kafka's offset commit is atomic per partition; commit happens after checkpoint complete\n4. **Idempotent processing:** Downstream operators must handle duplicate records (or use dedup)\n\n---\n\n### Thread Architecture\n\n**Kafka Side:**\n- **Producer:** User thread calls send() (async) or get() (sync); NetworkClient thread sends batches\n- **Consumer:** User thread calls poll() (blocking); Fetcher thread pre-fetches in background\n\n**Flink Source Side:**\n- **Main task thread:** Calls SourceOperator.emitNext() → SourceReader.pollNext()\n- **SplitFetcher threads:** SplitReader.fetch() (may block on I/O)\n- **Handoff via queue:** FutureCompletingBlockingQueue bridges async fetcher → sync main thread\n\n**Synchronization Points:**\n- Checkpoint barrier synchronizes across all operators\n- SourceReaderBase pauses fetcher threads during snapshotState()\n- RecordEmitter processes records before emitting (timestamp assignment, watermarks)\n\n---\n\n### Consumer Group Coordination Model\n\n**Kafka's GroupCoordinator:**\n1. Each consumer sends heartbeat (session.timeout.ms)\n2. If no heartbeat for timeout, consumer marked dead\n3. Rebalance triggered: ConsumerRebalanceListener callbacks\n4. PartitionAssignor (e.g., RangeAssignor) distributes partitions\n\n**Flink's Approach:**\n- KafkaSourceReader participates in consumer group if enabled\n- If partitions revoked: reader pauses polling\n- If partitions assigned: reader resumes from committed offset\n- Seamless for single-job reading; careful for multi-job consumer groups\n\n---\n\n### Capital Markets Use Case\n\nIn a capital markets trade ingestion pipeline:\n\n1. **Producer Side:**\n   - Exchange systems produce ProducerRecord<String, TradeMsg> with timestamp=exchange time\n   - Serializer converts TradeMsg (POJO) → JSON or Avro bytes\n   - KafkaProducer batches trades, sends with acks=all for durability\n   - Topic partitioned by symbol: \"AAPL\", \"MSFT\", \"GOOG\" → partition 0, 1, 2\n\n2. **Consumer Side (Flink):**\n   - KafkaSource reads all partitions\n   - SplitEnumerator creates: KafkaPartitionSplit(\"trades\", 0), KafkaPartitionSplit(\"trades\", 1), ...\n   - SplitEnumerator assigns: SourceReader 0 gets partitions 0-2, SourceReader 1 gets partitions 3-5\n   - KafkaSourceReader wraps KafkaConsumer<String, TradeMsg>\n   - Deserializer converts JSON → TradeMsg POJO\n   - RecordEmitter extracts timestamp, assigns watermark (Watermark(tradeMsg.timestamp()))\n\n3. **Checkpoint-Offset:**\n   - Every 60s: snapshotState() saves offset per partition\n   - After checkpoint: commitSync() writes to Kafka __consumer_offsets\n   - On restart: resume from last committed offset\n   - Exactly-once guarantee: no trades lost, no duplicates within Flink (downstream dedup for cross-system failures)\n\n4. **Risk Calculation:**\n   - Downstream operators read from KafkaSourceReader output\n   - Windowed aggregation (e.g., per-symbol volume in last minute)\n   - Watermark drives window closure\n   - Late trades dropped or side-output based on policy\n\n---\n\n## Dependency Diagram\n\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│  KAFKA BROKERS (Map, Window, Sink, etc.)             │\n│  (watermark alignment, state checkpointing, output)         │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n---\n\n## Summary\n\nThe Kafka-Flink streaming integration is a **three-layer architecture**:\n\n1. **Kafka Layer (Transport):** Produces/consumes bytes, manages replication, coordinates consumer groups, persists offsets.\n\n2. **Flink Connector Layer (Bridge):** KafkaSource/KafkaSourceReader wraps KafkaConsumer, discovers partitions as splits, maps Kafka's offset model to Flink's split+state model, applies custom deserialization.\n\n3. **Flink Core Layer (Semantics):** SourceReader/SplitEnumerator abstract the external system; SourceOperator integrates with Flink's checkpoint protocol; RecordEmitter transforms data and assigns timestamps/watermarks.\n\n**Critical Integration Point:** The checkpoint-to-offset commit flow ensures exactly-once semantics. SourceOperator.snapshotState() calls SourceReader.snapshotState(), which KafkaSourceReader translates to Kafka partition offsets. After checkpoint completion, notifyCheckpointComplete() triggers commitSync() to persist offsets to Kafka's __consumer_offsets topic. Recovery restores splits from state, seeks KafkaConsumer to those offsets, and resumes. This pattern is reusable for any external system (S3, databases, message queues) by implementing the SourceReader/SplitEnumerator interfaces.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":10,"cache_creation_input_tokens":498,"cache_read_input_tokens":99229,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":498},"output_tokens":1,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"ee5d4003-84ee-49a5-aea8-5fd0fcfb9b46","uuid":"978fc85a-7c20-420e-b8cb-483989753015"}

snapshotState(checkpointId) → CheckpointT\n\n**Connector Base Framework:**\n- `flink-connectors/flink-connector-base/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/connector/base/source/reader/SourceReaderBase.java` handleSplitsChanges(SplitsChange), wakeUp()\n- `flink-connectors/flink-connector-base/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/connector/base/source/reader/RecordEmitter.java` — Interface: emitRecord(E element, deserialize(byte[] message) → T; used for Kafka consumer record deserialization (custom serde boundary)\n\n**Runtime Integration:**\n- `flink-runtime/src/main/java/org/apache/flink/streaming/api/operators/SourceOperator.java` — Streaming operator that wraps SourceReader<OUT, SplitT>; calls sourceReader.snapshotState(checkpointId) in snapshotState() method (line 659)\n\n---\n\n## Dependency Chain & Data Flow\n\n### 1. Kafka Producer Path (Write Side)\n\n```\nUser Code\n    ↓\nProducerRecord<K, V>\n    ├─ topic: String\n    ├─ partition: Integer (optional)\n    ├─ key: K (optional)\n    ├─ value: V\n    ├─ timestamp: Long (optional)\n    └─ headers: Iterable<Header>\n    ↓\nSerializer<K> + Serializer<V>\n    ├─ serialize(String topic, T data) → byte[]\n    └─ configure(Map<String, ?> configs, boolean isKey)\n    ↓\nKafkaProducer<K, V>\n    ├─ send(ProducerRecord) → Future<RecordMetadata>\n    ├─ Internal: NetworkClient, Accumulator (batching)\n    ├─ Compression: snappy, lz4, zstd, gzip\n    └─ Transactional: initTransactions(), beginTransaction(), commitTransaction()\n    ↓\nKafka Broker (Topic → Partition → LogSegment)\n```\n\n**Key Contract:** Serializer.serialize() must be deterministic and idempotent. ProducerRecord includes timestamp (CREATE_TIME vs LOG_APPEND_TIME).\n\n---\n\n### 2. Kafka Consumer Path (Read Side)\n\n```\nKafka Broker (Partition → Fetch Offset Range)\n    ↓\nKafkaConsumer<K, V>\n    ├─ subscribe(Collection<String> topics) / assign(Collection<TopicPartition>)\n    ├─ poll(Duration timeout) → ConsumerRecords<K, V>\n    ├─ commitSync(Map<TopicPartition, OffsetAndMetadata>) [blocking]\n    ├─ commitAsync(Map<TopicPartition, OffsetAndMetadata>, Callback) [non-blocking]\n    ├─ Internal: NetworkClient, Fetcher, Deserializer\n    └─ Consumer Group Coordination: rebalance listener, committed offsets in __consumer_offsets topic\n    ↓\nDeserializer<K> + Deserializer<V>\n    ├─ deserialize(String topic, byte[] data) → T\n    ├─ deserialize(String topic, Headers headers, byte[] data) → T\n    └─ configure(Map<String, ?> configs, boolean isKey)\n    ↓\nConsumerRecord<K, V>\n    ├─ topic: String\n    ├─ partition: int\n    ├─ offset: long\n    ├─ key: K (optional, null if key was null on producer)\n    ├─ value: V\n    ├─ timestamp: long\n    ├─ timestampType: TimestampType (CREATE_TIME, LOG_APPEND_TIME)\n    ├─ serializedKeySize: int\n    ├─ serializedValueSize: int\n    └─ headers: Headers\n    ↓\nOffsetAndMetadata\n    ├─ offset: long\n    ├─ metadata: String (optional context)\n    └─ leaderEpoch: Optional<Integer> (for log truncation detection)\n    ↓\ncommitSync(Map<TopicPartition, OffsetAndMetadata>)\n    → Writes to __consumer_offsets topic (broker-side)\n    → Consumer group membership tracked in GroupCoordinator\n    ↓\nUser Code\n```\n\n**Key Contract:** Consumer.commitSync() is blocking. Offsets are only safe when committed. Consumer group rebalancing triggers ConsumerRebalanceListener callbacks.\n\n---\n\n### 3. Flink Source API Integration\n\n```\nSplitEnumerator<SplitT, CheckpointT>\n    ├─ Runs on JobManager (or Coordinator thread pool)\n    ├─ Discovers splits: handleSplitRequest(subtaskId, hostname)\n    ├─ Assigns splits: context.assignSplit(split, subtaskId)\n    ├─ Snapshots state: snapshotState(checkpointId) → CheckpointT\n    └─ Notifies completion: context.signalNoMoreSplits(subtaskId)\n    ↓\nSourceReader<T, SplitT extends SourceSplit>\n    ├─ Runs on TaskManager (per parallel subtask)\n    ├─ Adds splits: addSplits(List<SplitT>)\n    ├─ Polls records: pollNext(ReaderOutput<T>) → InputStatus\n    ├─ Snapshots state: snapshotState(checkpointId) → List<SplitT>\n    ├─ Signals completion: notifyNoMoreSplits()\n    └─ Availability: isAvailable() → CompletableFuture<Void>\n    ↓\nSourceReaderBase<E, T, SplitT, SplitStateT> (connector-base)\n    ├─ Manages: SplitFetcherManager<E, SplitT> (thread pool)\n    ├─ Buffers: FutureCompletingBlockingQueue<RecordsWithSplitIds<E>>\n    ├─ Emits: RecordEmitter<E, T, SplitStateT>.emitRecord()\n    └─ Poll loop:\n    │   ├─ SplitFetcher threads call SplitReader<E, SplitT>.fetch()\n    │   ├─ Records enqueued to elementsQueue\n    │   └─ Main thread calls pollNext() to drain queue → emitRecord()\n    ↓\nSplitReader<E, SplitT>\n    ├─ Reads from external source (e.g., KafkaConsumer)\n    ├─ fetch() → RecordsWithSplitIds<E> [may block]\n    ├─ handleSplitsChanges(SplitsChange) [non-blocking]\n    └─ wakeUp() [interrupt fetch() on blocking I/O]\n    ↓\nRecordEmitter<E, T, SplitStateT>\n    └─ emitRecord(E element, SourceOutput<T> output, SplitStateT splitState)\n        → Converts element to T\n        → Emits to SourceOutput with timestamp/watermark\n        → Updates splitState (e.g., offset tracking)\n    ↓\nReaderOutput<T> / SourceOutput<T>\n    └─ collect(T record)\n    └─ emitWatermark(Watermark)\n    ↓\nStreaming Task (SourceOperator)\n```\n\n**Key Contract:** SourceReader.pollNext() must be non-blocking. isAvailable() returns a future that completes when data is ready. Splits are immutable snapshots; SplitState tracks mutable position (e.g., offset).\n\n---\n\n### 4. Flink-Kafka Connector Bridge (Conceptual)\n\nThe Flink Kafka Connector (in apache/flink-connector-kafka) implements:\n\n```\nKafkaSource<K, V> implements Source<ConsumerRecord<K, V>>\n    ├─ Creates: KafkaSplitEnumerator\n    │   ├─ Uses: KafkaConsumer to discover partitions\n    │   ├─ Creates: KafkaPartitionSplit (topic, partition, startOffset, endOffset)\n    │   └─ Assigns splits to KafkaSourceReader instances\n    └─ Creates: KafkaSourceReader<K, V>\n        ├─ Wraps: KafkaConsumer<K, V>\n        ├─ Implements: SourceReaderBase or SplitReader\n        ├─ Deserializes: ConsumerRecord<K, V> via Kafka Deserializer<K>, Deserializer<V>\n        │   ↓ (Dual serde boundary:)\n        │   1. Kafka layer: Deserializer<K>, Deserializer<V>\n        │   2. Flink layer: DeserializationSchema<ConsumerRecord<K, V>> (optional wrapper)\n        ├─ Tracks: offset per partition in SplitState\n        └─ Emits: ConsumerRecord<K, V> to downstream\n```\n\n**The Dual Serialization Boundary:**\n- **Kafka Serde:** Deserializer<K> and Deserializer<V> convert bytes to typed objects (K, V)\n- **Flink Serde:** DeserializationSchema<T> further transforms ConsumerRecord<K, V> if needed (e.g., extract timestamp, watermark, schema evolution)\n\n---\n\n### 5. Checkpoint-Offset Integration\n\n```\nSourceOperator<OUT, SplitT> (Streaming Runtime)\n    ├─ Holds: SourceReader<OUT, SplitT>\n    ├─ Stores: ListState<byte[]> splitStates\n    └─ On checkpoint:\n        ↓\n        snapshotState(StateSnapshotContext context)\n            checkpointId = context.getCheckpointId()\n            readerState.update(sourceReader.snapshotState(checkpointId))\n            ↓\n            Flink writes readerState to RocksDB/JobManager state backend\n            ↓\n        sourceReader.snapshotState(checkpointId) [via SourceReaderBase]\n            →  SourceReaderBase.snapshotState()\n                ├─ Calls RecordEmitter.getState() to flush pending records\n                ├─ Collects all SplitState snapshots\n                │   └─ For KafkaSourceReader: offset per partition\n                └─ Returns List<SplitT> with serialized state\n            ↓\n        StateBackend (RocksDB, in-memory, external storage)\n            ↓\n        Checkpoint barrier propagates downstream\n            ↓\n        After checkpoint completes: notifyCheckpointComplete(checkpointId)\n            ├─ KafkaSourceReader extracts offsets from SplitState\n                └─ Calls: kafkaConsumer.commitSync(Map<TopicPartition, OffsetAndMetadata>)\n                    ├─ Topic: __consumer_offsets\n                    ├─ GroupId: Flink job's consumer group\n                    ├─ Stores: TopicPartition → (offset, metadata)\n                    └─ Metadata: checkpoint ID or Flink-specific context\n                ↓\n        On recovery:\n            readerState.get() → List<SplitT>\n            sourceReader.addSplits(splits)\n            ├─ KafkaSourceReader seeks to offsets from splits\n            └─ Resume from exactly those offsets\n```\n\n**Key Contract:**\n1. **Snapshot:** SourceReader.snapshotState(checkpointId) returns split state (offsets)\n2. **Barrier:** Checkpoint barrier ensures no in-flight records between snapshots\n3. **Commit:** Post-checkpoint, KafkaConsumer.commitSync() persists offsets to broker\n4. **Recovery:** On restart, Flink restores splits and seeks KafkaConsumer to committed offsets\n5. **Exactly-once:** Only offsets from completed checkpoints are committed to Kafka; failed checkpoints don't advance the committed offset\n\n---\n\n## Detailed Analysis\n\n### Producer Side (Data Ingestion)\n\n**Kafka's Responsibility:**\n- ProducerRecord encapsulates topic, key, value, partition, timestamp\n- Serializer interface defines contract for converting objects to bytes\n- KafkaProducer handles batching, compression, retries, and acknowledgments (acks=all, acks=1, acks=0)\n- Records are replicated to leader and replicas before ack\n\n**Timestamp Semantics:**\n- CREATE_TIME: Producer assigns timestamp; broker uses it as-is\n- LOG_APPEND_TIME: Broker overwrites with append timestamp (prevents clock skew)\n- Message format v2+ includes timestamp type and value in the record\n\n---\n\n### Consumer Side (Data Reading)\n\n**Kafka's Responsibility:**\n- Consumer group coordinates via broker's GroupCoordinator (heartbeat, rebalance)\n- Partitions assigned by PartitionAssignor (round-robin, range, sticky, cooperative)\n- Fetch protocol retrieves messages in offset order from each partition\n- Offset commit writes to __consumer_offsets (internal compacted topic)\n- ConsumerRecord preserves all metadata: offset, partition, timestamp, headers\n\n**Consumer Offset Management:**\n- Manual commit: commitSync() or commitAsync() with explicit offset\n- Automatic commit: enable.auto.commit=true, auto.commit.interval.ms\n- Rebalance behavior: ConsumerRebalanceListener.onPartitionsRevoked() (revoke offset), onPartitionsAssigned() (assign)\n- Log truncation detection: leaderEpoch in ConsumerRecord and OffsetAndMetadata\n\n---\n\n### Flink's Abstraction Layer\n\n**Flink Source API (Core Concepts):**\n\n1. **Splits:** Immutable work units discovered by SplitEnumerator\n   - For Kafka: KafkaPartitionSplit(topic, partition, startOffset, endOffset)\n   - Example: topic=\"events\", partition=0, startOffset=1000, endOffset=5000\n\n2. **SplitEnumerator:** Runs once, discovers all splits, assigns to readers\n   - Assigns splits to readers via SplitEnumeratorContext.assignSplit()\n   - Notifies completion: signalNoMoreSplits(subtaskId)\n   - Checkpoint: saves which splits are unassigned\n\n3. **SourceReader:** Runs per parallel subtask, reads assigned splits\n   - Non-blocking pollNext() method (returns InputStatus)\n   - Futures-based availability: isAvailable() → CompletableFuture<Void>\n   - Stateful: snapshotState() returns mutable state (e.g., current offset)\n\n4. **SourceReaderBase:** Helper class managing async fetch threads\n   - SplitFetcherManager: pool of fetcher threads, each with a SplitReader\n   - FutureCompletingBlockingQueue: bridges async fetch → sync poll\n   - RecordEmitter: transforms fetched elements to output type\n\n5. **RecordEmitter:** User-implemented record conversion\n   - Takes intermediate element E from SplitReader\n   - Converts to final type T and emits via SourceOutput\n   - Updates SplitState (e.g., offset) based on element\n\n---\n\n### Dual Serialization Boundary\n\n**Kafka Layer (Transport):**\n- Bytes → ConsumerRecord<K, V> via Kafka Deserializer<K>, Deserializer<V>\n- Deserializer.deserialize(String topic, byte[] data) is stateless\n- Low-level: only converts bytes to typed objects\n\n**Flink Layer (Semantic):**\n- ConsumerRecord<K, V> → custom type T via DeserializationSchema<T>\n- DeserializationSchema.deserialize(byte[] message) has access to full context\n- Optional: used for schema evolution, filtering, watermark assignment\n\n**Example Integration:**\n```java\n// Kafka deserializes: byte[] → (K key, V value)\nKafkaConsumer<String, JsonNode> consumer = new KafkaConsumer<>(\n    props,\n    new StringDeserializer(),\n    new JsonDeserializer()\n);\n\n// Flink's KafkaSourceReader extracts ConsumerRecord<String, JsonNode>\n// Then applies custom schema:\nnew DeserializationSchema<TradeRecord>() {\n    public TradeRecord deserialize(byte[] message) {\n        JsonNode json = mapper.readValue(message);\n        return new TradeRecord(json.get(\"id\"), json.get(\"price\"));\n    }\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n### Checkpoint-Offset Integration Deep Dive\n\n**Phase 1: Snapshot (During Checkpoint)**\n\n```\nJobManager issues checkpoint request\n    ↓ Barrier injected at source\nSourceOperator.snapshotState(StateSnapshotContext)\n    ├─ Calls: sourceReader.snapshotState(checkpointId)\n    │   └─ SourceReaderBase.snapshotState():\n    │       ├─ Pauses SplitFetcherManager threads (briefly)\n    │       ├─ Drains any buffered elements (elementsQueue)\n    │       ├─ Calls RecordEmitter.flushState()\n    │       └─ Collects List<SplitT> with current state\n    │           └─ For Kafka: KafkaPartitionSplit(topic, partition, offset)\n    │               where offset = highest offset processed + 1\n    ├─ Serializes List<SplitT> using SimpleVersionedSerializer<SplitT>\n    └─ Writes to ListState<byte[]> (state backend)\n    ↓\nStateBackend (RocksDB) writes snapshot to stable storage\n    ├─ Local: RocksDB (replica redundancy)\n    └─ Remote: HDFS, S3, DFS (for fault tolerance)\n    ↓\nCheckpoint reaches operator DAG\n    └─ All operators snapshot state\n    ↓\nMaster acknowledges checkpoint complete\n```\n\n**Phase 2: Commit (Post-Checkpoint)**\n\n```\nCoordinator calls: SourceOperator.notifyCheckpointComplete(checkpointId)\n    ↓\nSourceReaderBase.notifyCheckpointComplete(checkpointId)\n    ├─ Extracts offsets from recent splits\n    │   └─ KafkaPartitionSplit.splitId() = \"partition-X:offset-Y\"\n    ├─ Builds: Map<TopicPartition, OffsetAndMetadata>\n    │   ├─ TopicPartition(topic=\"events\", partition=0)\n    │   ├─ OffsetAndMetadata(offset=5001, metadata=\"checkpoint-123\")\n    │   └─ ... (one entry per partition being read)\n    └─ Calls: kafkaConsumer.commitSync(offsets)\n        ├─ Sends OffsetCommit request to broker\n        ├─ Broker writes to __consumer_offsets topic\n        └─ Returns when replicated (durability guarantee)\n    ↓\nConsumer group offset updated\n    └─ Next restart can resume from committed offset\n```\n\n**Phase 3: Recovery**\n\n```\nFlink TaskManager restarts (on failure)\n    ↓\nSourceOperator.initializeState(StateInitializationContext)\n    ├─ Restores ListState<byte[]> from state backend\n    ├─ Deserializes: List<SplitT>\n    │   └─ KafkaPartitionSplit(topic, partition, offset=5001)\n    ├─ Calls: sourceReader.addSplits(splits)\n    │   ├─ KafkaSourceReader.addSplits():\n    │   │   ├─ Creates KafkaConsumer if not exists\n    │   │   ├─ Calls: consumer.assign(topicPartitions)\n    │   │   ├─ Calls: consumer.seek(tp, offset)\n    │   │   └─ Resumes polling from offset\n    │   └─ SourceReaderBase enqueues to internal queue\n    ↓\nProcessing resumes from offset 5001\n    └─ No duplicate records (offset 5001 was not yet emitted before failure)\n```\n\n**Why This Works:**\n1. **Snapshots before emit:** The offset in the snapshot is the next-to-read offset (not yet emitted)\n2. **No double-counting:** If a record at offset 5000 was fetched but not emitted before failure, it's re-emitted after recovery\n3. **Atomic commits:** Kafka's offset commit is atomic per partition; commit happens after checkpoint complete\n4. **Idempotent processing:** Downstream operators must handle duplicate records (or use dedup)\n\n---\n\n### Thread Architecture\n\n**Kafka Side:**\n- **Producer:** User thread calls send() (async) or get() (sync); NetworkClient thread sends batches\n- **Consumer:** User thread calls poll() (blocking); Fetcher thread pre-fetches in background\n\n**Flink Source Side:**\n- **Main task thread:** Calls SourceOperator.emitNext() → SourceReader.pollNext()\n- **SplitFetcher threads:** SplitReader.fetch() (may block on I/O)\n- **Handoff via queue:** FutureCompletingBlockingQueue bridges async fetcher → sync main thread\n\n**Synchronization Points:**\n- Checkpoint barrier synchronizes across all operators\n- SourceReaderBase pauses fetcher threads during snapshotState()\n- RecordEmitter processes records before emitting (timestamp assignment, watermarks)\n\n---\n\n### Consumer Group Coordination Model\n\n**Kafka's GroupCoordinator:**\n1. Each consumer sends heartbeat (session.timeout.ms)\n2. If no heartbeat for timeout, consumer marked dead\n3. Rebalance triggered: ConsumerRebalanceListener callbacks\n4. PartitionAssignor (e.g., RangeAssignor) distributes partitions\n\n**Flink's Approach:**\n- KafkaSourceReader participates in consumer group if enabled\n- If partitions revoked: reader pauses polling\n- If partitions assigned: reader resumes from committed offset\n- Seamless for single-job reading; careful for multi-job consumer groups\n\n---\n\n### Capital Markets Use Case\n\nIn a capital markets trade ingestion pipeline:\n\n1. **Producer Side:**\n   - Exchange systems produce ProducerRecord<String, TradeMsg> with timestamp=exchange time\n   - Serializer converts TradeMsg (POJO) → JSON or Avro bytes\n   - KafkaProducer batches trades, sends with acks=all for durability\n   - Topic partitioned by symbol: \"AAPL\", \"MSFT\", \"GOOG\" → partition 0, 1, 2\n\n2. **Consumer Side (Flink):**\n   - KafkaSource reads all partitions\n   - SplitEnumerator creates: KafkaPartitionSplit(\"trades\", 0), KafkaPartitionSplit(\"trades\", 1), ...\n   - SplitEnumerator assigns: SourceReader 0 gets partitions 0-2, SourceReader 1 gets partitions 3-5\n   - KafkaSourceReader wraps KafkaConsumer<String, TradeMsg>\n   - Deserializer converts JSON → TradeMsg POJO\n   - RecordEmitter extracts timestamp, assigns watermark (Watermark(tradeMsg.timestamp()))\n\n3. **Checkpoint-Offset:**\n   - Every 60s: snapshotState() saves offset per partition\n   - After checkpoint: commitSync() writes to Kafka __consumer_offsets\n   - On restart: resume from last committed offset\n   - Exactly-once guarantee: no trades lost, no duplicates within Flink (downstream dedup for cross-system failures)\n\n4. **Risk Calculation:**\n   - Downstream operators read from KafkaSourceReader output\n   - Windowed aggregation (e.g., per-symbol volume in last minute)\n   - Watermark drives window closure\n   - Late trades dropped or side-output based on policy\n\n---\n\n## Dependency Diagram\n\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│  KAFKA BROKERS                                              │\n│  [Topic \"trades\" Partitions 0,1,2,...]                      │\n│  [__consumer_offsets (internal compacted topic)]            │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n                 ↑ Fetch / Commit offsets\n                 ↓ (poll & commitSync)\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│  KAFKA CLIENT LIBRARY (Client API) snapshotState() → List<SplitT>                          │\n│                                                              │\n│ SourceReader<T, SplitT> (interface)                         │\n│  ├─ ↓\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│  FLINK CORE SOURCE API (flink-core)                         │\n├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤\n│ SplitEnumerator<SplitT, CheckpointT>                        │\n│  ├─ handleSplitRequest(subtaskId)                           │\n│  ├─ addSplitsBack(splits, subtaskId)                        │\n│  └─ snapshotState(checkpointId) → CheckpointT              │\n│                                                              │\n│ DeserializationSchema<T>                                    │\n│  ├─ deserialize(byte[]) → T                                 │\n│  └─ isEndOfStream(T) → boolean                              │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n │\n├─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤\n│ SourceOperator<OUT, SplitT>                                 │\n│  ├─ Holds: SourceReader<OUT, SplitT>                        │\n│ notifyCheckpointComplete(checkpointId)                  │\n│  │   └─ calls kafkaConsumer.commitSync()                    │\n│  ├─ initializeState(StateInitializationContext)             │\n│  │   └─ │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n                 ↓ (output records to)\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│  DOWNSTREAM OPERATORS (Map, Window, Sink, etc.)             │\n│  (watermark alignment, state checkpointing, output)         │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n---\n\n## Summary\n\nThe Kafka-Flink streaming integration is a **three-layer architecture**:\n\n1. **Kafka Layer (Transport):** Produces/consumes bytes, manages replication, coordinates consumer groups, persists offsets.\n\n2. **Flink Connector Layer (Bridge):** KafkaSource/KafkaSourceReader wraps KafkaConsumer, discovers partitions as splits, maps Kafka's offset model to Flink's split+state model, applies custom deserialization.\n\n3. **Flink Core Layer (Semantics):** SourceReader/SplitEnumerator abstract the external system; SourceOperator integrates with Flink's checkpoint protocol; RecordEmitter transforms data and assigns timestamps/watermarks.\n\n**Critical Integration Point:** The checkpoint-to-offset commit flow ensures exactly-once semantics. SourceOperator.snapshotState() calls SourceReader.snapshotState(), which KafkaSourceReader translates to Kafka partition offsets. After checkpoint completion, notifyCheckpointComplete() triggers commitSync() to persist offsets to Kafka's __consumer_offsets topic. Recovery restores splits from state, seeks KafkaConsumer to those offsets, and resumes. This pattern is reusable for any external system (S3, databases, message queues) by implementing the SourceReader/SplitEnumerator interfaces.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

(2) Flink connector layer (apache/flink-connector-kafka) wraps KafkaConsumer, discovers partitions as SplitEnumerator splits, implements SplitReader to call consumer.poll(), maps Kafka offsets to Flink split state. (3) Flink core layer provides SourceReader/SplitEnumerator abstraction, SourceReaderBase helper with SplitFetcherManager for async fetching, RecordEmitter for timestamp/watermark assignment, SourceOperator for checkpoint integration. The checkpoint-offset integration is critical: SourceOperator.snapshotState() calls SourceReader.snapshotState() which returns List<SplitT> containing current offsets; after checkpoint completes, notifyCheckpointComplete() triggers Consumer.commitSync(Map<TopicPartition, OffsetAndMetadata>) to persist offsets. On recovery, splits are restored and KafkaConsumer.seek() resumes from committed offset. This pattern provides exactly-once semantics: snapshot = next-to-read offset, barrier prevents duplicates in Flink, commit after barrier ensures no re-processing. Dual serialization boundary: Kafka Deserializer<K>/Deserializer<V> converts bytes to typed objects (K, V); Flink DeserializationSchema<T> optionally wraps ConsumerRecord<K,V> for semantic transformation. Thread architecture: SplitFetcherManager runs fetcher threads calling SplitReader.fetch() (blocking on I/O), results enqueued to FutureCompletingBlockingQueue, main task thread calls SourceReader.pollNext() (non-blocking) to drain queue and emit records via RecordEmitter.\\\"\\n  }\\n}\\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}
